QUESTION
  Consider the Merger Agreement between "Sitel Worldwide Corporation" and "Sykes Enterprises, Incorporated"; Is there a Tail provision for acquisition proposals

ANSWER
  Keep’s policy says it uses a range of technical safeguards to protect your data, including “encryption and authentication tools,” “secure servers,” and “SSL technology… then encrypted within our databases” for payment information. It also promises not to sell or transfer your personal information to non‑affiliated third parties except when required by law or with your consent, and it will retain data only for limited periods, though it notes that “like other companies, we cannot guarantee 100 % the security or confidentiality of the information you provide to us.” These provisions indicate that the app takes reasonable security steps, but it does not promise absolute protection.

  1 = faithful   0 = unfaithful
  (a refusal asserts nothing unsupported, so it counts as faithful)

[case:01]

QUESTION
  Consider Kerber's Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document allow verbally conveyed information to be considered as Confidential Information?

ANSWER
  Yes. Excerpt 1 states that “Information initially furnished orally and identified by the disclosing party as confidential or proprietary at the time of disclosure will be confirmed by the disclosing party as Confidential Information in writing within thirty (30) days.” Thus verbal disclosures can become “Confidential Information” if they are identified as such when spoken and later confirmed in writing within the 30‑day period.

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  [Excerpt 1 — Kerber_Non_Disclosure_Agreement.txt]

    ________ _________________________________ _________________________________ OF THE

    ; and B. It may be necessary or desirable for each party to disclose to the other pa
    rty certain confidential or proprietary technical information in order to enable dis
    cussions to freely take place between them concerning the subject matter mentioned a
    bove. NOW THEREFORE in consideration of the premises, covenants and agreements herei
    n set forth, the parties agree as follows: 1. For the purpose of this Agreement, “Co
    nfidential Information” means information, technical or commercial, whether in visua
    l or machine readable form, received by one party from the other which is marked “Co
    nfidential” or “Proprietary”, or which would logically be considered confidential or
     proprietary in view of its relationship to the whole disclosure. Information initia
    lly furnished orally and identified by the disclosing party as confidential or propr
    ietary at the time of disclosure will be confirmed by the disclosing party as Confid
    ential Information in writing within thirty (30) days. 2. The receiving party will n
    ot use, manufacture or sell any document, schematic, drawing, photograph, specificat
    ion, specimen or any other material making up any part of the Confidential Informati
    on, or use any Confidential Information as a basis for the design or creation of any
     items or other means without the prior written consent of the disclosing party. 3. 
    For a period of five (5) years from the date of receiving it, all Confidential Infor
    mation will be maintained in confidence by the receiving party, will not be disclose
    d to any third party or to any persons employed

QUESTION
  Consider the Non-Exclusive License Agreement between The Johns Hopkins University and Virtuoso Surgical, Inc. for Commercial Development of Surgical Products; What are the audit rights under this contract?

ANSWER
  The agreement gives Johns Hopkins University the right to audit the company’s records:
  > “9.1 Audit: JHU shall have the right to audit any and all Company records related to this Agreement.”【Excerpt 1】

========================================================================================
[case:04]
========================================================================================

QUESTION
  Consider the Consulting Agreement between Aduro Biotech, Inc. and IREYA B.V.; Does this contract include an exclusivity agreement?

ANSWER
  The excerpts contain no provision that obligates the consultant to work exclusively for Aduro. The only limitation on the consultant’s other work is a representation that the consultant “will not… engage in… consulting services… that would present a material conflict with any of the provisions of this Agreement” (see Excerpt 5, §8(d)), which is a conflict‑of‑interest restriction, not an exclusivity clause. Therefore, the contract does not include an exclusivity agreement.

QUESTION
  Consider the Software License and Maintenance Agreement between Garman Routing Systems, Inc. and Sparkling Spring Water Group Limited; Are any of the licenses granted under this contract irrevocable or perpetual?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement provides that (a) the source‑code escrow license is “a **perpetual, fully paid, non‑exclusive license**” to use the deposited codes, but Sparkling may rely on it only if the agreement is terminated because of Garman’s default or if Garman ceases support (Section 16(b)). And (b) if Sparkling terminates the agreement due to Garman’s default, “the license herein granted shall continue, but shall be **converted to a perpetual license**” with no further fees (Section 17(c)).

QUESTION
  Consider the Acquisition Agreement between Parent "LVMH Moët Hennessy-Louis Vuitton SE" and Target "Tiffany & Co."; What is the Definition of "Knowledge"

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement contains an exclusivity provision. Article IV, Section 4.1 states:
  > “**Exclusivity.** Except as specifically provided in the Distribution Agreement, Conformis shall be prohibited from developing or assisting another in developing, or causing another to develop, Patient‑Specific Instrumentation for Off‑The‑Shelf Knee Implants for any Third Party in the field of orthopedics until January 1, 2032 …”【Excerpt 4】.

[case:07]

QUESTION
  Consider the Co-Branding Agreement between Dassault Systemes and PlanetCAD Inc.; Are the licenses granted under this contract non-transferable?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement provides a **non‑transferable** license for each party’s marks. As the contract states:
  > “On or before the Launch Date, each party shall grant the other party a **non‑exclusive, non‑transferable, revocable** right to use their approved Marks, for the sole purpose of advertising, marketing, promotion and sale of the Co‑Branded Service.”【Excerpt 3†L3-L5】
  Thus, at least the mark‑use licenses are expressly non‑transferable. (Other licenses, such as the limited hosting license, are not described as non‑transferable in the excerpts.)

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)

[case:08]

QUESTION
  Consider the Internet Channel Cooperation Agreement between Beijing Baidu Netcom Science and Technology Co., Ltd., China Online Housing (Hong Kong) Co., Ltd., and Beijing Yisheng Leju Information Services Co., Ltd.; Is there an anti-assignment clause in this contract?

ANSWER
  The agreement gives several limited, royalty‑free licenses:
  * VerticalNet grants LeadersOnline a **non‑exclusive, non‑transferable link license** to link to the VerticalNet Site (“2.3. Link License…grant…a non‑exclusive, non‑transferable, royalty‑free right and license to link to the VerticalNet Site.”).
  * VerticalNet also grants LeadersOnline a **mark license** to use the VerticalNet Mark in an approved design (“2.2. Mark License…grant…a non‑exclusive, non‑transferable, royalty‑free right and license…to utilize a VerticalNet Mark…”).
  * VerticalNet provides LeadersOnline an **access license** to the Resume Bank for contacting candidates (“5.1. Access License…VerticalNet hereby grants to LeadersOnline a non‑exclusive, non‑transferable right and license to access the Resume Bank.”).
  In return, LeadersOnline grants VerticalNet an **information license** to use, copy or modify the LeadersOnline Mark, descriptive text and URL for employer spotlights (“4.3. Information License…LeadersOnline hereby grants VerticalNet a non‑exclusive, non‑transferable, royalty‑free right and license…to use, copy or modify the LeadersOnline Mark, text describing LeadersOnline and the URL address…”).

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)

QUESTION
  Consider the Software License and Maintenance Agreement between Garman Routing Systems, Inc. and Sparkling Spring Water Group Limited; Are any of the licenses granted under this contract irrevocable or perpetual?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement contains a covenant not to sue (or “challenge”) the other party’s rights.  As the license states:
  > “The Village Media Company shall not, and shall cause its Affiliates and their respective permitted sublicensees not to, whether during the Term or thereafter, **challenge** (a) the rights of PFHOF … (b) the validity of any PFHOF Work … (c) PFHOF’s right to grant rights … or (d) the validity, legality, or enforceability of this Agreement.”【Excerpt 3】

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION
  Consider the Co-Promotion Agreement between Dova Pharmaceuticals, Inc. and Valeant Pharmaceuticals North America LLC; Is there uncapped liability under this contract?

ANSWER
  Yes. The excerpts contain no provision that caps the parties’ liability; the indemnification clauses require each party to “defend, indemnify and hold harmless” the other “from and against all Claims and all associated Losses” without any limitation (see Excerpt 5, §§ 11.1‑11.2). Because no liability‑cap language appears in the excerpts, the contract leaves liability uncapped.

========================================================================================
[case:13]
========================================================================================

QUESTION
  Consider the Merger Agreement between "Sitel Worldwide Corporation" and "Sykes Enterprises, Incorporated"; Is there a Tail provision for acquisition proposals

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreements require the Receiving Party to give the Disclosing Party notice before any legally‑required disclosure. For example, the AGProjects NDA states that the Receiving Party “shall provide the DISCLOSING PARTY with prompt written notice of any such request or requirement so that the DISCLOSING PARTY may seek a protective order…,” and the DBT Mutual NDA adds that disclosure “is required … so long as the other party is provided notice of such requirement prior to any such disclosure.”

[case:14]

QUESTION
  Consider the Tabun Kitchen Investments' Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document state that Confidential Information shall only include technical information?

ANSWER
  No. The Tabun Kitchen Investments NDA defines “Confidential Information” broadly, covering both technical and non‑technical matters. It states that Confidential Information includes “software, utilities, solutions, designs, techniques, methods, … data … forecasts, business plans, strategies, financial statements, records, … customer lists … and other business or technical information or trade secrets.”【Excerpt 1 — TabunKitchenInvestments‑NDA.txt, paragraph 1】.

  Is every claim in the answer supported by these excerpts?
  1 = faithful   0 = unfaithful
  (a refusal asserts nothing unsupported, so it counts as faithful)



========================================================================================
[case:15]
========================================================================================

QUESTION
  Consider JBF's Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document allow the Receiving Party to share some Confidential Information with their employees?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement provides a **non‑transferable** license for each party’s marks. As the contract states:
  > “On or before the Launch Date, each party shall grant the other party a **non‑exclusive, non‑transferable, revocable** right to use their approved Marks, for the sole purpose of advertising, marketing, promotion and sale of the Co‑Branded Service.”【Excerpt 3†L3-L5】
  Thus, at least the mark‑use licenses are expressly non‑transferable. (Other licenses, such as the limited hosting license, are not described as non‑transferable in the excerpts.)

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)

========================================================================================
[case:16]

QUESTION
  Consider Kerber's Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document allow verbally conveyed information to be considered as Confidential Information?

ANSWER
  “Material Adverse Effect” means, with respect to the Company and its Subsidiaries taken as a whole, any change, event, occurrence, circumstance, condition or effect that, individually or together with all other effects, would or would reasonably be expected to be materially adverse to the financial condition (including assets and liabilities), business, operations or results of operations of the Company and its Subsidiaries, **provided that none of the following shall be deemed in and of themselves, either alone or in combination, to constitute a Material Adverse Effect**:
  - changes in general economic conditions or financial, credit, foreign‑exchange, securities, currency, capital or other financial markets;
  - changes generally affecting the industry in which the Company and its Subsidiaries operate;
  - changes in applicable legal requirements;
  - changes in GAAP or other accounting principles;
  - national or international political conditions, wars, terrorism, etc.;
  - epidemics, natural disasters or similar crises;
  - changes in trading volume or price of the Company’s stock (unless underlying the change); and
  - any failure, in and of itself, to meet market revenue or earnings expectations or internal projections.
  *Excerpt 7, “Material Adverse Effect” definition.*

QUESTION
  Consider the Co-Branding Agreement between Lucent Technologies Inc. and mPhase Technologies Inc.; Is there a cap on liability under this contract?

ANSWER
  The agreement does not set a monetary cap on liability; it only limits the types of damages the parties can recover.  As the contract states, “Neither Party shall be liable to the other for special, incidental, or consequential damages, even if such …”【Excerpt 4】. No other provision imposes a dollar‑amount limit on liability.

QUESTION
  Consider the Cooperation Agreement between the City of Fort Stockton, Texas and STW Resources Holding Corp. for Water Well Development; Does this contract include any right of first refusal, right of first offer, or right of first negotiation?

  I don't know.

[case:19]

QUESTION
  Consider the Co-Branding Agreement between Skype Communications, Skype Technologies, TOM Online (BVI) Limited, TOM Online Inc., and Tel-Online Limited; Is there an anti-assignment clause in this contract?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement contains a restriction on assignment:
  > “This Agreement may not be assigned by a party to this Agreement to any other Person without the express written approval of the other parties to this Agreement and any attempt at assignment in violation of this section shall be null and void. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Skype or Skype Holding may assign this Agreement … in the event of a merger, reorganization or sale of all or substantially all of Skype's or Skype Holding's assets … provided that written notice … is delivered … and the assignee assumes all the responsibilities and obligations provided herein.” (Excerpt 4, Section 14.4)

========================================================================================
[case:20]
========================================================================================

QUESTION
  Consider FullStory's Mutual Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document permit the Receiving Party to create a copy of some Confidential Information under certain circumstances?

ANSWER
  The FullStory Mutual NDA does not contain any provision that allows the Recipient to make copies of Confidential Information. The excerpt states that the Recipient “will not use any Confidential Information except to the extent necessary for the internal purpose… Recipient will not disseminate or disclose any Confidential Information…” and provides no language permitting copying. Therefore, the agreement does not permit the Receiving Party to create a copy under any circumstance.

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

QUESTION
  Consider "23andMe"'s privacy policy; What about the Fiduciary exception to the No-Shop Clause

ANSWER
  “Material Adverse Effect” means, with respect to the Company and its Subsidiaries taken as a whole, any change, event, occurrence, circumstance, condition or effect that, individually or together with all other effects, would or would reasonably be expected to be materially adverse to the financial condition (including assets and liabilities), business, operations or results of operations of the Company and its Subsidiaries, **provided that none of the following shall be deemed in and of themselves, either alone or in combination, to constitute a Material Adverse Effect**:
  - changes in general economic conditions or financial, credit, foreign‑exchange, securities, currency, capital or other financial markets;
  - changes generally affecting the industry in which the Company and its Subsidiaries operate;
  - changes in applicable legal requirements;
  - changes in GAAP or other accounting principles;
  - national or international political conditions, wars, terrorism, etc.;
  - epidemics, natural disasters or similar crises;
  - changes in trading volume or price of the Company’s stock (unless underlying the change); and
  - any failure, in and of itself, to meet market revenue or earnings expectations or internal projections.
  *Excerpt 7, “Material Adverse Effect” definition.*

QUESTION
  Consider Grindrod SA's Non-Disclosure Agreement; Does the document state that Confidential Information shall only include technical information?

ANSWER
  No. Grindrod SA’s definition of “Confidential Information” is not limited to technical information; it expressly covers a broad range of data, including “all technical, commercial, procurement requirements, purchasing, manufacturing, customer lists, investors, employees, business and contractual relationships, business forecasts, sales and merchandising, and marketing plans” among others【Excerpt 1†L1-L4】. Thus the document does not restrict Confidential Information to technical matters only.

QUESTION
  Consider the Mutual Non-Disclosure Agreement between Wayne Fueling Systems and Unknown Party; Does the document require that all Confidential Information be expressly identified by the Disclosing Party?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement defines Confidential Information as information that “is or has been disclosed by the Disclosing Party… (i) in writing … and is clearly marked ‘Confidential’ or ‘Proprietary’; or (ii) orally or visually, and then followed within ten (10) working days thereafter with a disclosure complying with the requirements of clause (i) above.”【Excerpt 1 — wayne‑fueling‑systems‑mutual‑non‑disclosure‑agreement‑final.txt】. This makes explicit identification (by marking or a subsequent written notice) a condition for information to be treated as confidential.

QUESTION
  Consider the Acquisition Agreement between Parent "SANOFI" and Target "TRANSLATE BIO, INC."; What is the Type of Consideration

ANSWER
  Keep’s policy says it uses a range of technical safeguards to protect your data, including “encryption and authentication tools,” “secure servers,” and “SSL technology… then encrypted within our databases” for payment information. It also promises not to sell or transfer your personal information to non‑affiliated third parties except when required by law or with your consent, and it will retain data only for limited periods, though it notes that “like other companies, we cannot guarantee 100 % the security or confidentiality of the information you provide to us.” These provisions indicate that the app takes reasonable security steps, but it does not promise absolute protection.

QUESTION
  Consider the Consulting Agreement between Aduro Biotech, Inc. and IREYA B.V.; Does this contract include an exclusivity agreement?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement contains an exclusivity provision. Article IV, Section 4.1 states:
  > “**Exclusivity.** Except as specifically provided in the Distribution Agreement, Conformis shall be prohibited from developing or assisting another in developing, or causing another to develop, Patient‑Specific Instrumentation for Off‑The‑Shelf Knee Implants for any Third Party in the field of orthopedics until January 1, 2032 …”【Excerpt 4】.

[case:29]

QUESTION
  Consider the Media License Agreement between National Football Museum, Inc. and HOF Village Media Group, LLC; Is there a covenant not to sue included in this contract?

ANSWER
  Yes. The agreement contains a covenant not to sue (or “challenge”) the other party’s rights.  As the license states:
  > “The Village Media Company shall not, and shall cause its Affiliates and their respective permitted sublicensees not to, whether during the Term or thereafter, **challenge** (a) the rights of PFHOF … (b) the validity of any PFHOF Work … (c) PFHOF’s right to grant rights … or (d) the validity, legality, or enforceability of this Agreement.”【Excerpt 3】

EXCERPTS PROVIDED (10)
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
